<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We show that a fixed deterministic Turing machine with one writable tape and head can be simulated in $`O(T^{2/5}\mathop{\mathrm{polylog}}\nolimits (T+2))`$ work-space bits when a binary time cap <i>T</i> ≥ 2 is supplied. The simulator computes the finite-control and halting outcome by time <i>T</i>; its running time is unrestricted. The result allows a fixed number of read-only input heads and requires a fixed accessor that supplies every initial writable and read-only symbol within distance <i>T</i> of the relevant head origin in polylogarithmic space. This improves the square-root space exponent for one-tape machines, answering Williams's question for this model.

- **claim-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Fix a deterministic machine with one writable tape and head, a fixed number of read-only input heads, unit movement and fixed origins for all heads, cap-independent symbol functions, and a fixed accessor as above. For every public input $x$ and supplied binary cap $T\ge2$ for which the access condition holds, its finite-control and halting outcome by time $T$ can be computed deterministically in $\widetilde O(T^{2/5})$ work-space bits, excluding storage for the read-only input. Simulation time is unrestricted, and the final writable tape need not be materialized. If the access condition holds for $(x,T)$ at every $T\ge2$, and the machine halts on $x$ after $t$ steps without a supplied running time, its outcome can be computed in $\widetilde O((t+2)^{2/5})$ work-space bits.
  - Explanation: Computes the capped finite-control and halting outcome for the specified fixed machine and accessor; repeated caps handle an unknown halting time.
- **claim-lem-field** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-field)): Given an integer $\Delta\ge1$ bounded polynomially in $T$, one can uniformly construct a field $\mathbb F$ of characteristic two with $\|\mathbb F\|>\Delta$. Field elements have $O(\log T)$ bits. A description of the field, enumeration of its elements, its arithmetic, and inversion of nonzero elements all require $\operatorname{polylog}T$ space.
  - Explanation: Builds a sufficiently large characteristic-two field with space-efficient enumeration and arithmetic.
- **claim-lem-homogenization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-homogenization)): For every total function $f:(\{0,1\}^d)^a\to\{0,1\}$, where $a$ is fixed, there is a polynomial $\widetilde f$ on $(\mathbb F^{d+1})^a$ that agrees with $f$ on augmented Boolean arguments and is homogeneous of degree $d$ separately in every argument. If a coordinate of $f$ is computable in $O(d\operatorname{polylog}T)$ space from its Boolean arguments, its extension can be evaluated at supplied field vectors in that space, with no recursive word evaluation. The same statement applies coordinatewise to augmented word-valued functions.
  - Explanation: Extends total Boolean functions by separately homogeneous lookup polynomials, including the augmented output coordinate.
- **claim-lem-top-coefficient** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-top-coefficient)): Let $L$ be an integer with $0\le L<\|\mathbb F\|$, choose any $L+1$ distinct elements $S_L\subseteq\mathbb F$, and set $$\omega_L(s)=\left(\prod_{u\in S_L\setminus\{s\}}(s-u)\right)^{-1} \qquad(s\in S_L).$$ For every univariate polynomial $p$ of degree at most $L$, $$\label{eq:top-coefficient} [X^L]p(X)=\sum_{s\in S_L}\omega_L(s)p(s).$$ If $Q$ is homogeneous of degree $L$, then, for arbitrary field vectors $a,v$, $$\label{eq:translation-extraction} \sum_{s\in S_L}\omega_L(s)Q(a+sv)=Q(v).$$ Choosing $S_L$ as the first $L+1$ elements in the uniform field enumeration, its points and weights can be enumerated and recomputed in $\operatorname{polylog}T$ space when $L$ is polynomially bounded in $T$.
  - Explanation: Extracts a top coefficient and a homogeneous translated value by weighted field evaluations.
- **claim-lem-weighted-evaluation** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-evaluation)): Under the preceding assumptions, the augmented value of a word $V$ can be computed deterministically in $$\label{eq:weighted-evaluation-space} O\bigl((d+W(V)+1)\operatorname{polylog}T\bigr)$$ work space, in addition to separately stored immutable auxiliary data. More strongly, a pool of exactly $p=\max(3,r+1)$ registers in $\mathbb F^{d+1}$ supports a procedure $\mathsf{Add}(V,\lambda,t)$, for every $\lambda\in\mathbb F$ and target index $t$, with the following contract for *every* initial assignment to the entire pool: $$\label{eq:add-contract} R_t^{\mathrm{out}}=R_t^{\mathrm{in}}+\lambda\overline{v(V)}, \qquad R_j^{\mathrm{out}}=R_j^{\mathrm{in}}\quad(j\ne t).$$ The space bound <a href="#eq:weighted-evaluation-space" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weighted-evaluation-space">[eq:weighted-evaluation-space]</a> includes this shared pool and all temporary and suspended storage.
  - Explanation: Evaluates an acyclic word recurrence using shared registers, restoring every non-target register from arbitrary entry contents.
- **claim-lem-visits** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-visits)): Some offset has at most $T/b$ block crossings in the capped computation. For that offset at most $R=\lceil1+T/b\rceil$ visits reach the halt or timeout. For every offset there are total maps on raw Boolean words $$\operatorname{sel}_a:\{0,1\}^c\longrightarrow\{1,\ldots,N\}, \qquad \operatorname{Visit}_a: \{0,1\}^c\times\{0,1\}^{b\ell_\Sigma} \longrightarrow \{0,1\}^c\times\{0,1\}^{b\ell_\Sigma}.$$ On input $(z,B)$, $B$ is interpreted as block $\operatorname{sel}_a(z)$. A visit changes only that block, agrees with the capped computation on represented actual data, and is computable in $O((b+1)\operatorname{polylog}T)$ work bits on explicit arguments.
  - Explanation: Averages boundary crossings over offsets and defines total local visits on raw controller and block arguments.
- **claim-lem-local-replay** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-local-replay)): Fix an integer $0\le m\le b$. For a raw controller list $Z=(z_0,\ldots,z_m)$ and a raw block $B\in\{0,1\}^{b\ell_\Sigma}$, define $$\operatorname{Replay}_i(Z,B)=(B^{\mathrm{out}},p_i)$$ by the following bounded scan. Initialize a buffer to $B$ and $p_i=1$. For $t=1,\ldots,m$, if $\operatorname{sel}_a(z_{t-1})=i$, apply $\operatorname{Visit}_a$ to $z_{t-1}$ and the buffer, retain its new buffer, and conjoin to $p_i$ the bitwise equality of its raw $c$-bit output with $z_t$. Continue the scan after a failed check. Visits with a different selector leave this buffer and flag unchanged. The buffer and flag are total Boolean functions of $Z,B$, using $O((b+(m+1)c+1)\operatorname{polylog}T)$ local workspace and no word-evaluation calls.* *From any fixed raw $z_0$ and raw blocks $B_1,\ldots,B_N$, define a reference evolution that applies the same total visit serially to its selected block and leaves the other blocks unchanged. This global $N$-block evolution specifies semantics only; the simulator never stores or evaluates the tuple. For a proposed list with this $z_0$, all replay flags equal one if and only if the list is the unique raw controller list of the reference evolution. When they equal one, each returned buffer is the corresponding reference block after $m$ visits.
  - Explanation: Replays each block independently and characterizes the unique consistent raw controller transcript.
- **claim-lem-factored-history** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-factored-history)): For $0\le j<k$, each round update $H_{e,j}\mapsto H_{e,j+1}$ is a factored rule of Lemma <a href="#lem:weighted-evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weighted-evaluation">5</a>, with distinguished child $H_{e,j}$, other children $A_{e,1},\ldots,A_{e,N}$, and guess length $g=kc\le d$.
  - Explanation: Factors a round update into a guessed controller suffix and epoch-start block replay predicates.
- **claim-lem-epoch-weight** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-epoch-weight)): Every dependency path from the final history has weight $$O(E(k+kc+1))=O(Ek\log(T+2)).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds dependency-path weight by charging at most one suspended round guess per epoch.
- **claim-prop-multitape** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-multitape)): Fix a deterministic machine with a fixed number of ordinary writable tapes and read-only input heads, unit movement and fixed origins for all heads, cap-independent symbol functions, and a fixed accessor. For every public input $x$ and supplied binary cap $T\ge2$ satisfying the access condition, its finite-control and halting outcome admits a deterministic simulation in $\widetilde O(\sqrt T)$ work-space bits, excluding read-only input storage. Simulation time is unrestricted.
  - Explanation: Gives a separate square-root-space simulation for a fixed number of writable tapes under the accessor condition.
- **claim-cor-space-hierarchy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-space-hierarchy)): For every space-constructible $s(n)\ge n$ and every fixed $0<\epsilon<5/2$, $$\mathrm{DSPACE}(s(n))\not\subseteq \mathrm{TIME}_{1w,\mathrm{ro}}\!\left(s(n)^{5/2-\epsilon}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Separates the stated deterministic space class from the one-writable-tape time class, assuming a space-constructible bound at least the input length and the stated epsilon range.
- **claim-cor-bounded-space-halting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-bounded-space-halting)): The language $H$ is complete for $\mathrm{DSPACE}(n)$ under logarithmic-space many-one reductions of linear output length. If $N$ denotes its input length, then for every fixed $0<\epsilon<5/2$, $$H\notin \mathrm{TIME}_{1w,\mathrm{ro}}\!\left(N^{5/2-\epsilon}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Establishes linear deterministic-space completeness of the defined bounded-space halting language and class nonmembership in the stated one-writable-tape time regime.

          "exact_quote": "For every total function $f:(\\{0,1\\}^d)^a\\to\\{0,1\\}$, where $a$ is fixed, there is a polynomial $\\widetilde f$ on $(\\mathbb F^{d+1})^a$ that agrees with $f$ on augmented Boolean arguments and is homogeneous of degree $d$ separately in every argument. If a coordinate of $f$ is computable in $O(d\\operatorname{polylog}T)$ space from its Boolean arguments, its extension can be evaluated at supplied field vectors in that space, with no recursive word evaluation. The same statement applies coordinatewise to augmented word-valued functions.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-137.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-top-coefficient",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be an integer with $0\\le L<|\\mathbb F|$, choose any $L+1$ distinct elements $S_L\\subseteq\\mathbb F$, and set $$\\omega_L(s)=\\left(\\prod_{u\\in S_L\\setminus\\{s\\}}(s-u)\\right)^{-1}\n \\qquad(s\\in S_L).$$ For every univariate polynomial $p$ of degree at most $L$, $$\\label{eq:top-coefficient}\n [X^L]p(X)=\\sum_{s\\in S_L}\\omega_L(s)p(s).$$ If $Q$ is homogeneous of degree $L$, then, for arbitrary field vectors $a,v$, $$\\label{eq:translation-extraction}\n \\sum_{s\\in S_L}\\omega_L(s)Q(a+sv)=Q(v).$$ Choosing $S_L$ as the first $L+1$ elements in the uniform field enumeration, its points and weights can be enumerated and recomputed in $\\operatorname{polylog}T$ space when $L$ is polynomially bounded in $T$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-137.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-top-coefficient",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be an integer with $0\\le L<|\\mathbb F|$, choose any $L+1$ distinct elements $S_L\\subseteq\\mathbb F$, and set $$\\omega_L(s)=\\left(\\prod_{u\\in S_L\\setminus\\{s\\}}(s-u)\\right)^{-1}\n \\qquad(s\\in S_L).$$ For every univariate polynomial $p$ of degree at most $L$, $$\\label{eq:top-coefficient}\n [X^L]p(X)=\\sum_{s\\in S_L}\\omega_L(s)p(s).$$ If $Q$ is homogeneous of degree $L$, then, for arbitrary field vectors $a,v$, $$\\label{eq:translation-extraction}\n \\sum_{s\\in S_L}\\omega_L(s)Q(a+sv)=Q(v).$$ Choosing $S_L$ as the first $L+1$ elements in the uniform field enumeration, its points and weights can be enumerated and recomputed in $\\operatorname{polylog}T$ space when $L$ is polynomially bounded in $T$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-137.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weighted-evaluation",
        "exact_quote": "Under the preceding assumptions, the augmented value of a word $V$ can be computed deterministically in $$\\label{eq:weighted-evaluation-space}\n O\\bigl((d+W(V)+1)\\operatorname{polylog}T\\bigr)$$ work space, in addition to separately stored immutable auxiliary data. More strongly, a pool of exactly $p=\\max(3,r+1)$ registers in $\\mathbb F^{d+1}$ supports a procedure $\\mathsf{Add}(V,\\lambda,t)$, for every $\\lambda\\in\\mathbb F$ and target index $t$, with the following contract for *every* initial assignment to the entire pool: $$\\label{eq:add-contract}\n R_t^{\\mathrm{out}}=R_t^{\\mathrm{in}}+\\lambda\\overline{v(V)},\n \\qquad\n R_j^{\\mathrm{out}}=R_j^{\\mathrm{in}}\\quad(j\\ne t).$$ The space bound <a href=\"#eq:weighted-evaluation-space\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-evaluation-space\">[eq:weighted-evaluation-space]</a> includes this shared pool and all temporary and suspended storage.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-137.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-weighted-evaluation",
          "exact_quote": "Under the preceding assumptions, the augmented value of a word $V$ can be computed deterministically in $$\\label{eq:weighted-evaluation-space}\n O\\bigl((d+W(V)+1)\\operatorname{polylog}T\\bigr)$$ work space, in addition to separately stored immutable auxiliary data. More strongly, a pool of exactly $p=\\max(3,r+1)$ registers in $\\mathbb F^{d+1}$ supports a procedure $\\mathsf{Add}(V,\\lambda,t)$, for every $\\lambda\\in\\mathbb F$ and target index $t$, with the following contract for *every* initial assignment to the entire pool: $$\\label{eq:add-contract}\n R_t^{\\mathrm{out}}=R_t^{\\mathrm{in}}+\\lambda\\overline{v(V)},\n \\qquad\n R_j^{\\mathrm{out}}=R_j^{\\mathrm{in}}\\quad(j\\ne t).$$ The space bound <a href=\"#eq:weighted-evaluation-space\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-evaluation-space\">[eq:weighted-evaluation-space]</a> includes this shared pool and all temporary and suspended storage.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-137.1"

      "exact_statement": "Under the preceding assumptions, the augmented value of a word $V$ can be computed deterministically in $$\\label{eq:weighted-evaluation-space}\n O\\bigl((d+W(V)+1)\\operatorname{polylog}T\\bigr)$$ work space, in addition to separately stored immutable auxiliary data. More strongly, a pool of exactly $p=\\max(3,r+1)$ registers in $\\mathbb F^{d+1}$ supports a procedure $\\mathsf{Add}(V,\\lambda,t)$, for every $\\lambda\\in\\mathbb F$ and target index $t$, with the following contract for *every* initial assignment to the entire pool: $$\\label{eq:add-contract}\n R_t^{\\mathrm{out}}=R_t^{\\mathrm{in}}+\\lambda\\overline{v(V)},\n \\qquad\n R_j^{\\mathrm{out}}=R_j^{\\mathrm{in}}\\quad(j\\ne t).$$ The space bound <a href=\"#eq:weighted-evaluation-space\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-evaluation-space\">[eq:weighted-evaluation-space]</a> includes this shared pool and all temporary and suspended storage.",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-137.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-visits",
        "exact_quote": "Some offset has at most $T/b$ block crossings in the capped computation. For that offset at most $R=\\lceil1+T/b\\rceil$ visits reach the halt or timeout. For every offset there are total maps on raw Boolean words $$\\operatorname{sel}_a:\\{0,1\\}^c\\longrightarrow\\{1,\\ldots,N\\},\n \\qquad\n \\operatorname{Visit}_a:\n \\{0,1\\}^c\\times\\{0,1\\}^{b\\ell_\\Sigma}\n \\longrightarrow \\{0,1\\}^c\\times\\{0,1\\}^{b\\ell_\\Sigma}.$$ On input $(z,B)$, $B$ is interpreted as block $\\operatorname{sel}_a(z)$. A visit changes only that block, agrees with the capped computation on represented actual data, and is computable in $O((b+1)\\operatorname{polylog}T)$ work bits on explicit arguments.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-replay",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $0\\le m\\le b$. For a raw controller list $Z=(z_0,\\ldots,z_m)$ and a raw block $B\\in\\{0,1\\}^{b\\ell_\\Sigma}$, define $$\\operatorname{Replay}_i(Z,B)=(B^{\\mathrm{out}},p_i)$$ by the following bounded scan. Initialize a buffer to $B$ and $p_i=1$. For $t=1,\\ldots,m$, if $\\operatorname{sel}_a(z_{t-1})=i$, apply $\\operatorname{Visit}_a$ to $z_{t-1}$ and the buffer, retain its new buffer, and conjoin to $p_i$ the bitwise equality of its raw $c$-bit output with $z_t$. Continue the scan after a failed check. Visits with a different selector leave this buffer and flag unchanged. The buffer and flag are total Boolean functions of $Z,B$, using $O((b+(m+1)c+1)\\operatorname{polylog}T)$ local workspace and no word-evaluation calls.*\n\n*From any fixed raw $z_0$ and raw blocks $B_1,\\ldots,B_N$, define a reference evolution that applies the same total visit serially to its selected block and leaves the other blocks unchanged. This global $N$-block evolution specifies semantics only; the simulator never stores or evaluates the tuple. For a proposed list with this $z_0$, all replay flags equal one if and only if the list is the unique raw controller list of the reference evolution. When they equal one, each returned buffer is the corresponding reference block after $m$ visits.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-137.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

Initialize a buffer to $B$ and $p_i=1$. For $t=1,\\ldots,m$, if $\\operatorname{sel}_a(z_{t-1})=i$, apply $\\operatorname{Visit}_a$ to $z_{t-1}$ and the buffer, retain its new buffer, and conjoin to $p_i$ the bitwise equality of its raw $c$-bit output with $z_t$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **symbol source** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $\sigma_h(x,q)$ for the symbol at displacement $q$ from that origin on public input $x$.
- **access condition** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We say that the *access condition holds for $(x,T)$* if, for every tag $h$ and integer $\|q\|\le T$, $\mathsf{Sym}(x,h,T,q)$ halts with $\sigma_h(x,q)$ using at most $C_0\log^{C_1}(T+2)$ work-space bits.
- **soft space notation** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We write $\widetilde O(f(T))$ for $O(f(T)\log^C(T+2))$, with a fixed constant $C$.
- **augmented Boolean word** ([source](#sec:evaluation)): For $v\in\{0,1\}^d$, write $$\overline v=(v_1,\ldots,v_d,1).$$
- **physical tape block** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For an integer width $b\ge1$ polynomially bounded in $T$, and offset $a\in\{0,\ldots,b-1\}$, physical block $v\in\mathbb Z$ is $[a+vb,a+(v+1)b-1]$.
- **reachable interval** ([source](#eq:block-count)): Put the writable origin at zero and write $I_T=[-T,T]\cap\mathbb Z$.
- **common word width** ([source](#eq:word-width)): Use the concrete common width $$\label{eq:word-width} d=\max\{1,b\ell_\Sigma,(b+1)c\} =O((b+1)\log(T+2))$$ for the following Boolean words.
- **deterministic space class** ([source](#sec:hierarchy)): Write $\mathrm{DSPACE}(s)$ for ordinary deterministic $O(s(n))$ work-space bits with an endmarked read-only input tape whose head stays between the endmarkers.
- **space constructibility** ([source](#sec:hierarchy)): We use the standard space-constructibility convention that a fixed machine can mark exactly $s(n)$ work cells on every input of length $n$; constant-factor variants give the same statements.
- **bounded-space halting language** ([source](#sec:hierarchy)): Let $H$ consist of the encodings $\langle M,x,1^k\rangle$ such that $\|M\|\le k$ and $M(x)$ halts after visiting at most $k$ work cells; malformed codes are outside $H$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-thm-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-epoch-weight-2): Computes the capped finite-control and halting outcome for the specified fixed machine and accessor; repeated caps handle an unknown halting time. -> Computes the capped finite-control and halting outcome for the specified fixed machine and accessor; repeated caps handle an unknown halting time.
- **step-lem-field** [claim](#stmt-lem-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-field): Builds a sufficiently large characteristic-two field with space-efficient enumeration and arithmetic. -> Builds a sufficiently large characteristic-two field with space-efficient enumeration and arithmetic.
- **step-lem-homogenization** [claim](#stmt-lem-homogenization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-homogenization): Extends total Boolean functions by separately homogeneous lookup polynomials, including the augmented output coordinate. -> Extends total Boolean functions by separately homogeneous lookup polynomials, including the augmented output coordinate.
- **step-lem-top-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-lem-top-coefficient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-top-coefficient): Extracts a top coefficient and a homogeneous translated value by weighted field evaluations. -> Extracts a top coefficient and a homogeneous translated value by weighted field evaluations.
- **step-lem-weighted-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-evaluation): Evaluates an acyclic word recurrence using shared registers, restoring every non-target register from arbitrary entry contents. -> Evaluates an acyclic word recurrence using shared registers, restoring every non-target register from arbitrary entry contents.
- **step-lem-visits** [claim](#stmt-lem-visits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-visits): Averages boundary crossings over offsets and defines total local visits on raw controller and block arguments. -> Averages boundary crossings over offsets and defines total local visits on raw controller and block arguments.
- **step-lem-local-replay** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-replay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-replay): Replays each block independently and characterizes the unique consistent raw controller transcript. -> Replays each block independently and characterizes the unique consistent raw controller transcript.
- **step-lem-factored-history** [claim](#stmt-lem-factored-history) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-factored-history): Factors a round update into a guessed controller suffix and epoch-start block replay predicates. -> Factors a round update into a guessed controller suffix and epoch-start block replay predicates.
- **step-lem-epoch-weight** [claim](#stmt-lem-epoch-weight) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-epoch-weight): Bounds dependency-path weight by charging at most one suspended round guess per epoch. -> Bounds dependency-path weight by charging at most one suspended round guess per epoch.
- **step-prop-multitape** [claim](#stmt-prop-multitape) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-multitape): Gives a separate square-root-space simulation for a fixed number of writable tapes under the accessor condition. -> Gives a separate square-root-space simulation for a fixed number of writable tapes under the accessor condition.
- **step-cor-space-hierarchy** [claim](#stmt-cor-space-hierarchy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-space-hierarchy): Separates the stated deterministic space class from the one-writable-tape time class, assuming a space-constructible bound at least the input length and the stated epsilon range. -> Separates the stated deterministic space class from the one-writable-tape time class, assuming a space-constructible bound at least the input length and the stated epsilon range.
- **step-cor-bounded-space-halting** [claim](#stmt-cor-bounded-space-halting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-bounded-space-halting): Establishes linear deterministic-space completeness of the defined bounded-space halting language and class nonmembership in the stated one-writable-tape time regime. -> Establishes linear deterministic-space completeness of the defined bounded-space halting language and class nonmembership in the stated one-writable-tape time regime.

Initialize a buffer to $B$ and $p_i=1$. For $t=1,\\ldots,m$, if $\\operatorname{sel}_a(z_{t-1})=i$, apply $\\operatorname{Visit}_a$ to $z_{t-1}$ and the buffer, retain its new buffer, and conjoin to $p_i$ the bitwise equality of its raw $c$-bit output with $z_t$. Continue the scan after a failed check. Visits with a different selector leave this buffer and flag unchanged. The buffer and flag are total Boolean functions of $Z,B$, using $O((b+(m+1)c+1)\\operatorname{polylog}T)$ local workspace and no word-evaluation calls.*\n\n*From any fixed raw $z_0$ and raw blocks $B_1,\\ldots,B_N$, define a reference evolution that applies the same total visit serially to its selected block and leaves the other blocks unchanged. This global $N$-block evolution specifies semantics only; the simulator never stores or evaluates the tuple. For a proposed list with this $z_0$, all replay flags equal one if and only if the list is the unique raw controller list of the reference evolution. When they equal one, each returned buffer is the corresponding reference block after $m$ visits.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-extraction**: Weighted evaluation at distinct field points extracts the top coefficient of a bounded-degree polynomial.
- **I-registers**: A word evaluation translates its target register by the fixed semantic value and restores all other registers, regardless of their entry contents.
- **I-simulation**: The simulator reports capped finite control and halting outcome under the symbol-access condition, without materializing the final writable tape.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-time**: Treat the space simulation as a running-time improvement. -> Recomputation and interpolation are permitted to take unrestricted time.
- **A-multitape**: Apply the factored round rule to joint visits on ordinary writable tapes. -> A joint transition can couple independently supplied symbols so its successful pairs do not factor by block.
- **A-augmentation**: Replace the homogeneous extension of a constant Boolean predicate by a literal constant. -> Shifted field arguments can have an augmentation coordinate that is zero.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The top-coefficient identity supplies a finite constructive interpolation procedure in characteristic two. The checked interpolation kernel does not establish the full simulator or its asymptotic space bound.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of polynomial cases with degree L, coefficients, distinct field points and field modulus.
# PRECONDITIONS: Characteristic-two field modulus is one of 3, 7, 11; 0 <= L < field size; exactly L+1 distinct points.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of extracted top coefficients, or REJECT for a malformed case.
# TIME: O(L^2 m^2 + L 2^m m^2) bit operations per case with m-bit field encodings.
# SPACE: O(L m) bits apart from the supplied input and serialized output.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact python-flint integers, XOR polynomial arithmetic over characteristic two.
# FAILURE: Malformed interpolation arguments return REJECT; unsupported field moduli return REJECT.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz

def extract(case):
    mod = case.get('mod')
    degree = case.get('L')
    co = case.get('coeff')
    points = case.get('points')
    if type(mod) is not int or mod not in (3, 7, 11):
        return 'REJECT'
    m = mod.bit_length()-1
    size = 1 << m
    if type(degree) is not int or not 0 <= degree < size:
        return 'REJECT'
    if not isinstance(co, list) or len(co) != degree+1:
        return 'REJECT'
    if not isinstance(points, list) or len(points) != degree+1:
        return 'REJECT'
    if any(type(x) is not int or not 0 <= x < size for x in co+points) or len(set(points)) != len(points):
        return 'REJECT'

    def mul(a, b):
        z = fmpz(0)
        while b:
            if b & 1:
                z = fmpz(int(z) ^ a)
            b >>= 1
            a <<= 1
            if a & size:
                a ^= mod
        return int(z)

    def inv(a):
        for candidate in range(1, size):
            if mul(a, candidate) == 1:
                return candidate
        raise ValueError('zero denominator')

    result = 0
    for s in points:
        value = 0
        for coefficient in reversed(co):
            value = mul(value, s) ^ coefficient
        denominator = 1
        for u in points:
            if u != s:
                denominator = mul(denominator, s ^ u)
        weight = inv(denominator)
        result ^= mul(weight, value)
    return result

print(json.dumps([extract(case) for case in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The deterministic simulator can guide an implementation that reports capped finite-control and halting outcomes while reusing storage. Its transfer to concrete software is conditional on preserving the machine and accessor contract; practical execution performance is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no game-state or interactive simulation contract is provided. Capped deterministic tape simulation supplies only finite-control and halting outcomes with unrestricted simulation time. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no practical diagnostic or analysis tool is specified. The capped outcome theorem alone supplies no useful running-time guarantee for an engineering tool. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The paper states deterministic capped simulation and a reusable evaluator for factored word recurrences. The evaluator translates a target register by a fixed semantic word and restores all other registers from arbitrary entry contents. |
| data_structures | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The shared register pool and contiguous stack make storage reuse part of the evaluator contract. Stack occupancy follows weighted dependency paths, and disposable lookup scratch is not live across recursive word calls. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper gives a sequential work-space analysis and no accelerator architecture, parallel schedule or device resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: finite fields and interpolation serve exact deterministic evaluation; no cryptographic primitive, adversary model or security guarantee is specified. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the stated computation is fixed and deterministic, with no training rule, learned model or statistical performance contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: balancing block, epoch and round scales analyses this simulation estimate; it does not specify an optimisation solver or an operations-research application. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: factorization over tape blocks is a sequential consistency argument, with no distributed communication, fault or concurrency contract. |
| other (computational_complexity) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The space hierarchy consequences separate deterministic space classes from the stated one-writable-tape time classes. The bounded-space halting language has the stated completeness under logarithmic-space reductions of linear output length. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The simulation assumes a fixed deterministic machine with one writable tape and head, unit movement, fixed head origins, cap-independent symbol functions, and a fixed accessor. Blocked use: ['Applying the main simulation bound to machines with unrestricted writable heads or arbitrary movement.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): Symbol access must halt within the stated workspace bound at every coordinate in the cap radius, including speculative local computations. Blocked use: ['Checking access only at coordinates reached by the actual run.', 'Using an unproved symbol interface for virtual inputs.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The capped theorem assumes access for its supplied input and cap; the unknown-running-time clause requires access at every trial cap. Blocked use: ['Inferring the unknown-running-time clause from access at a single cap.']
- **L4** (formal_scope): The bound measures work-space bits and excludes read-only input storage; the requested output is finite control and the halting outcome. Blocked use: ['Treating the bound as total storage including the public input.', 'Requiring the simulator to materialize the final writable tape.']
- **L5** (formal_scope): The theorem supplies no useful simulation-time bound and cannot be iterated as a time improvement. Blocked use: ['Using the space exponent as a running-time guarantee.', 'Iterating the result to claim faster execution.']
- **L6** (approximation): The asymptotic bound retains polylogarithmic factors; no log-free endpoint is asserted. Blocked use: ['Claiming a log-free workspace bound at exponent $2/5$.']
- **L7** (counterexample_scope): Joint inspection of independently supplied writable bits can prevent factorization into predicates on individual blocks. This obstructs the stated factorization rather than all multitape simulations. Blocked use: ['Extending the factored round rule to arbitrary multitape transitions.', 'Turning the factorization obstruction into an impossibility theorem for all simulations.']
- **L8** (formal_scope): The multitape comparison is a separate square-root space simulation and does not establish a new general-multitape exponent. Blocked use: ['Advertising the main one-writable-head exponent for general multitape computation.']
- **L9** (formal_scope): Varying the block, epoch, and round scales constrains only the displayed construction estimate. Blocked use: ['Claiming that the balancing calculation proves optimality or a lower bound on other simulations.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The evaluator requires fixed semantic word values, an acyclic dependency graph, uniform navigation and local Boolean routines that make no word-evaluation calls. Blocked use: ['Applying the shared-register contract to values dependent on mutable register contents.', 'Ignoring local computation or navigation costs.']
- **L11** (edge_case): Homogeneous extensions of globally constant predicates retain augmentation coordinates, including shifted arguments with zero augmentation. Blocked use: ['Replacing homogeneous constant-one lookup extensions by literal $1$.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): Top-coefficient extraction requires distinct interpolation points and a degree below the field cardinality. Blocked use: ['Using repeated points or allowing zero interpolation denominators.', 'Extracting the stated coefficient from a polynomial above the specified degree.']
- **L13** (edge_case): Local replay is total on raw inputs and continues scanning after a failed check; consistency is characterized with the initial raw controller fixed. Blocked use: ['Stopping replay at the first failed check when implementing the stated total rule.', 'Interpreting a passing suffix without preserving the initial controller and preceding history.']
- **L14** (hypothesis): The hierarchy separation assumes a space-constructible bound at least the input length and a fixed epsilon in the stated open range. Blocked use: ['Dropping space constructibility or the input-length lower bound.', 'Claiming the excluded exponent endpoints.']
- **L15** (formal_scope): The hierarchy consequences are class nonmembership in the one-writable-head model, rather than a bound at every input length. Blocked use: ['Claiming per-length lower bounds, general multitape bounds, random-access bounds, or a logarithmic endpoint.']
- **L16** (formal_scope): A specified sequential output bit can be monitored without storing the output stream. Blocked use: ['Treating the monitored-bit remark as a guarantee to retain the complete output stream.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 137 (`lean/docs/137.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Simulating One-Tape Time in Two-Fifths-Power Space](../../preprints/Simulating-One-Tape-Time-in-Two-Fifths-Power-Space-September-25-2026/article.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives two-fifths-power space simulation of a fixed deterministic machine with one writable tape and finitely many read-only input heads. Heads move at most one cell per step, initial contents are independent of the time bound, and contents and input symbols must be accessible in polylogarithmic space. Given a binary time cap $T\ge2$, the simulator returns the source's finite-control outcome and halting flag using $O(T^{2/5}(\log(T+2))^C)$ space.
>
> A second simulator needs no time cap: on an input that halts at time $t$, it returns the halted outcome using $O((t+2)^{2/5}(\log(t+2))^C)$ space. Simulation time is unrestricted. General multitape simulation, hierarchy consequences, and bounded-space-halting results are outside these selected statements.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Finite-machine statements of the capped and unknown-time one-writable-tape
simulation theorem. Work space counts the bits in every visited writable cell;
public endmarked input tapes are read-only. -/

namespace Fifths

inductive Dir
  | L | N | R
  deriving DecidableEq

instance : Fintype Dir := ⟨{.L, .N, .R}, by intro d; cases d <;> simp⟩

namespace Dir
@[simp] def delta : Dir → ℤ
  | L => -1
  | N => 0
  | R => 1
end Dir

inductive Mark (A : Type)
  | left
  | sym (a : A)
  | right
  deriving DecidableEq, Fintype

namespace Mark
variable {A : Type}
def see (w : List A) : ℕ → Mark A
  | 0 => left
  | p+1 =>
    match w[p]? with
    | some a => sym a
    | none => right

def walk (w : List A) (p : ℕ) : Dir → ℕ
  | .N => p
  | .L => p - 1
  | .R => if w.length < p then p else p + 1
end Mark

inductive Runs {σ Y : Type} (f : σ → Y ⊕ σ) (P : σ → Prop) :
    σ → σ → Y → Prop
  | stop {s y} (ok : P s) (eqn : f s = Sum.inl y) : Runs f P s s y
  | step {s s' t y} (ok : P s) (eqn : f s = Sum.inr s')
      (tail : Runs f P s' t y) : Runs f P s t y

structure Inputs (X J : Type) where
  publicWord : List X
  arg : J → List Bool

structure Readers (J : Type) where
  px : ℕ := 0
  parg : J → ℕ := fun _ => 0

structure Reads (X J : Type) where
  sx : Mark X
  sarg : J → Mark Bool

structure Advances (J : Type) where
  dx : Dir := .N
  darg : J → Dir := fun _ => .N

namespace Readers
variable {X J : Type}
def home : Readers J := {}
def see (r : Readers J) (i : Inputs X J) : Reads X J :=
  ⟨Mark.see i.publicWord r.px, fun j => Mark.see (i.arg j) (r.parg j)⟩
def walk (r : Readers J) (i : Inputs X J) (a : Advances J) : Readers J :=
  ⟨Mark.walk i.publicWord r.px a.dx, fun j => Mark.walk (i.arg j) (r.parg j) (a.darg j)⟩
end Readers

structure Tape (A : Type) where
  pos : ℤ
  sym : ℤ → A

structure WriteMove (A : Type) where
  sym : A
  dir : Dir

namespace Tape
variable {A : Type}
def init (a : A) : Tape A := ⟨0, fun _ => a⟩
def see (w : Tape A) : A := w.sym w.pos
def perform (w : Tape A) (m : WriteMove A) : Tape A :=
  ⟨w.pos + m.dir.delta, Function.update w.sym w.pos m.sym⟩
end Tape

structure TAction (Q J K : Type) (W : K → Type) where
  next : Q
  advance : Advances J
  moves : ∀ k : K, WriteMove (W k)

structure TM (X J Y : Type) where
  Q : Type
  [finiteQ : Finite Q]
  K : Type
  [tapes : Fintype K]
  W : K → Type
  [symbols : ∀ k, Fintype (W k)]
  blank : ∀ k, W k
  start : Q
  table : Q → Reads X J → ((k : K) → W k) → Y ⊕ TAction Q J K W

attribute [instance] TM.finiteQ TM.tapes TM.symbols

namespace TM
variable {X J Y : Type} (M : TM X J Y)

structure Cfg where
  state : M.Q
  ro : Readers J
  work : ∀ k, Tape (M.W k)

def initial : M.Cfg :=
  ⟨M.start, .home, fun k => .init (M.blank k)⟩

def step (i : Inputs X J) (s : M.Cfg) : Y ⊕ M.Cfg :=
  match M.table s.state (s.ro.see i) (fun k => (s.work k).see) with
  | Sum.inl y => .inl y
  | Sum.inr a =>
    .inr ⟨a.next, s.ro.walk i a.advance, fun k => (s.work k).perform (a.moves k)⟩

structure Window where
  l : M.K → ℕ
  h : M.K → ℕ

def width (k : M.K) : ℕ := max 1 (Fintype.card (M.W k) - 1).size

namespace Window
variable {M}
def ok (w : M.Window) (s : M.Cfg) : Prop :=
  ∀ k, -(w.l k : ℤ) ≤ (s.work k).pos ∧ (s.work k).pos ≤ (w.h k : ℤ)
open BigOperators
def bits (w : M.Window) : ℕ :=
  ∑ k, (w.l k + w.h k + 1) * M.width k
end Window

def Within (i : Inputs X J) (y : Y) (S : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ w : M.Window, w.bits ≤ S ∧ ∃ t, Runs (M.step i) w.ok M.initial t y
end TM

end Fifths

namespace Fifths

structure SingleAct (Q A : Type) (r : ℕ) where
  out : Q
  write : WriteMove A
  others : Fin r → Dir

structure Single (X : Type) where
  Q : Type
  [states : Fintype Q]
  A : Type
  [symbols : Fintype A]
  beginState : Q
  halted : Q → Bool
  heads : ℕ
  program : Q → A → (Fin heads → A) → SingleAct Q A heads
  sensor : List X → Option (Fin heads) → ℤ → A

attribute [instance] Single.states Single.symbols

namespace Single
variable {X : Type} (M : Single X)
instance : Nonempty M.A := ⟨M.sensor [] none 0⟩
instance : Nonempty M.Q := ⟨M.beginState⟩
abbrev Tag := Option (Fin M.heads)

structure At where
  state : M.Q
  tape : Tape M.A
  ro : Fin M.heads → ℤ

def move (q : M.Q) (s : M.A) (r : Fin M.heads → M.A) :
    SingleAct M.Q M.A M.heads :=
  if M.halted q then
    ⟨q,⟨s,.N⟩,fun _ => .N⟩
  else M.program q s r

def initial (x : List X) : M.At :=
  ⟨M.beginState,⟨0,M.sensor x none⟩, fun _ => 0⟩

def next (x : List X) (s : M.At) : M.At :=
  let t := M.move s.state s.tape.see (fun i => M.sensor x (some i) (s.ro i))
  ⟨t.out,s.tape.perform t.write,fun i => s.ro i + (t.others i).delta⟩

def atTime (M : Single X) (x : List X) : ℕ → M.At :=
  Nat.rec (M.initial x) (fun _ s => M.next x s)

abbrev Outcome := M.Q × Bool
def finish (q : M.Q) : M.Outcome :=
  (q, M.halted q)
def outcome (x : List X) (n : ℕ) : M.Outcome :=
  M.finish (M.atTime x n).state

def Inside (t : ℕ) (q : ℤ) : Prop := -(t:ℤ) ≤ q ∧ q ≤ (t:ℤ)
end Single

end Fifths

namespace Fifths

namespace Entry
noncomputable def code {A : Type} [Fintype A] (a : A) : ℕ :=
  (Fintype.equivFin A a).val
end Entry

def binaryIn {X J : Type} (pub : List X) (f : J → ℕ) : Inputs X J :=
  ⟨pub, fun j => (f j).bits⟩

def files {X : Type} (x : List X) : Inputs X Empty :=
  ⟨x, Empty.elim⟩

namespace Single
open Entry
variable {X : Type} (M : Single X)

inductive Arg
  | cap | tag | minus | magnitude
  deriving DecidableEq

instance : Fintype Arg :=
  ⟨{.cap, .tag, .minus, .magnitude}, by intro a; cases a <;> simp⟩
abbrev Sym := TM X Arg M.A

noncomputable def arg (T : ℕ) (h : M.Tag) (p : ℤ) : Arg → ℕ
  | .cap => T
  | .tag => code h
  | .minus => (decide (p < 0)).toNat
  | .magnitude => p.natAbs
end Single

namespace Cost
noncomputable def Log (T : ℕ) := Real.log (T+2 : ℝ) / Real.log 2
noncomputable def exponent := (2:ℝ)/5
end Cost

namespace Single
open Cost
variable {X : Type} (M : Single X)
def Access (A : M.Sym) (p q : ℝ) (x : List X) (T : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (h : M.Tag) (l : ℤ), Inside T l →
    ∃ s : ℕ, (s:ℝ) ≤ p * (Log T)^q ∧
      A.Within (binaryIn x (M.arg T h l)) (M.sensor x h l) s
end Single

end Fifths

namespace Fifths.Single
open Entry Cost
variable {X : Type} (M : Single X)

theorem capped [Fintype X] (A : M.Sym) (p q : ℝ) (hp : 1 ≤ p) (_hq : 1 ≤ q) :
    ∃ (N : TM X Unit M.Outcome) (e : ℕ) (c : ℝ), 0 < c ∧
      ∀ (x : List X) (T : ℕ), 2 ≤ T → M.Access A p q x T →
        ∃ b : ℕ, N.Within (binaryIn x (fun _ => T)) (M.outcome x T) b ∧
          (b:ℝ) ≤ c*(T:ℝ)^exponent*(Log T)^e := by
  sorry

theorem unknown [Fintype X] (A : M.Sym) (p q : ℝ) (hp : 1 ≤ p) (_hq : 1 ≤ q) :
    ∃ (N : TM X Empty M.Outcome) (e : ℕ) (c : ℝ), 0 < c ∧
      ∀ (x : List X), (∀ T, 2 ≤ T → M.Access A p q x T) →
        ∀ (t : ℕ), (M.outcome x t).2 = true →
          ∃ b, N.Within (files x) (M.outcome x t) b ∧
            (b:ℝ) ≤ c*((t:ℝ)+2)^exponent*(Log t)^e := by
  sorry

end Fifths.Single

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We show that a fixed deterministic Turing machine with one writable tape and head can be simulated in $`O(T^{2/5}\mathop{\mathrm{polylog}}\nolimits (T+2))`$ work-space bits when a binary time cap <i>T</i> ≥ 2 is supplied. The simulator computes the finite-control and halting outcome by time <i>T</i>; its running time is unrestricted. The result allows a fixed number of read-only input heads and requires a fixed accessor that supplies every initial writable and read-only symbol within distance <i>T</i> of the relevant head origin in polylogarithmic space. This improves the square-root space exponent for one-tape machines, answering Williams's question for this model.
